  Thought for 29s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found it. The unit dump shows the group is read correctly (j, k, group→ADD, w)
  — but the bracketed array is [nil, nil, TRUE, nil]: holes, because
  unbracketed factors store nil. Then table.insert(bracketed, 1, false) shifts
  by #bracketed, and # on a table with holes is allowed to stop at a border
  before the TRUE — here it shifted only the prefix, leaving TRUE at index 3
  (describing k) instead of 4 (the group). The bug was latent all along: the old
  maybe_cell promoted any leaf, so a misplaced TRUE on k did nothing — until
  the ruling made leaf promotion conditional. Fixing the root:

  Thought for 6s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● 98/98 green. Your report was real — and no, f was not being imagined a
  function. The undeclared reading was built per the ruling (MUL(f, CELL(x)));
  the bug was one array slot away from it.

  What was actually wrong — a latent misalignment in build_product
  (scripts/mexpr_ast.lua), exposed by the ruling change:

  - Each factor records whether it came from brackets: bracketed[k] = 
    from_brackets. For ordinary factors that value is nil — so the array had
    holes.
  - A signed term prepends its sign coefficient with table.insert(bracketed, 1, 
    false), which shifts by #bracketed — and on a table with holes, Lua may
    answer with a border short of the end. Here it shifted only the prefix,
    leaving the group's true one slot left: on f, not around x. Hence
    MUL(CELL(f), x).
  - It was latent because the old rule promoted any leaf — a stray flag on a
    leaf did nothing. The moment promotion became conditional on position, the
    stray flag started wrapping the wrong factor.

  The fix is one line: bracketed[#factors] = from_brackets or false — the array
  stays a sequence, # is exact, the flag describes the factor it was read for.

  Verified end to end (98/98, including your exact formula now pinned by an
  assertion):

  - undeclared: a(b+f(x)) parses as MUL(a, ADD(b, MUL(+1, f, CELL(x)))) — cell
    on the x — and distributes to ab+af(x), parens given back
  - af(x) vs afx: two trees, MUL(a, f, CELL(x)) vs MUL(a, f, x)
  - declared: af(x) is MUL(a, CALL("f(),(1)", x)), round-trips

  One housekeeping note: your running main.exe loaded the old scripts — restart
  it to pick up the fix.

  Thought for 5s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● 98/98. Root cause and fix:

  Why the box was dead — handle_input returned early while the box had no live
  formula container (scripts/editor_formula.lua):

  if not state_formula.formula then
      return false
  end

  An empty box never builds one on its own, so no keystroke had anywhere to
  land. The paste is the only path that ever built a formula into an empty box —
  which is exactly why your dance worked: the paste created the container,
  deleting a character never removed it, and from then on the box had something
  to type into.

  Where it came from — the 2026-09-07 design, where an empty formula box was
  paste-only ("you type it in a text box and bring it over"). The 2026-09-15
  EDIT-mode work made a filled unlocked box a full mathbox, but never extended
  that to the empty state. The file's own header still claimed a paste was "the
  ONLY way content gets in" — that's amended too.

  The fix — an unlocked empty box builds an empty mathbox on its first frame of
  input: mformula.new, the exact construction a typed $$ inserts in a text box,
  caret ready, so the first keystroke lands. Two properties worth stating:

  - input runs only for the active box, so no other empty box on the page grows
    a container it doesn't need
  - the committed string stays nil while empty — an empty box still saves and
    reloads as empty; the container is where a first character arrives, not
    content

  Suite is 98/98 (the harness can't drive the input path, so that's regression
  cover, not this fix). Restart the app, click the empty box, and type.

● 98/98. Your report was in the recorder — input_history.log line 2964:

  transform: distribute cannot be drawn yet: nothing to copy for this name - it
  was never drawn in the source

  So the distribute ran; the write-back refused, and that refusal shows only in
  the log (and F6).

  Root cause — your definition declares f bare (arity 0 — the name slot is f,
  not f(x)). That reading makes f(x) a product of a declared-name reference and
  a CELL — the tree my test_ast_view update recorded this morning. The
  free-letter branch of the parse tags its glyph, but a resolved name goes
  through build_named, which tagged nothing: no ink was ever recorded for that
  reference. Distribute then makes a fresh copy of it, the writer finds nothing
  to copy, and refuses.

  Fix (scripts/mexpr_ast.lua) — the parse now tags the base glyph with the node
  it resolved to, the same two tags the free-letter branch writes: ast_id (a
  click on a declared name now says which node it is) and ast_draws (the writer
  can copy the name's own ink — your f, dress included, not a re-render). Tagged
  only when the base is one glyph's ink; a quoted or digit-run base is several
  glyphs, and copying one of them would be a wrong drawing rather than a
  refusal.

  Verified — reproduced first (the test refused with the exact live message),
  now the bare-declared a(b+f(x)) distributes, writes ab+af(x), and round-trips.
  98/98. Restart the app to pick it up.

  Two side notes:

  - The comment above tag_ast still says "a resolved name's letters -
    build_named is not handed the glyphs" — that sentence is now stale. Per your
    instruction I did not touch it; flagging it for your decision along with
    the comment cleanup you're thinking about.
  - CLAUDE.md now states the rule you gave: DESIGN.md is the only trustworthy
    design record ("The only trutable design is DESIGN.md"), a code comment
    describes what the code does and rules nothing.

  Thought for 5s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● 99/99. Declared names now wear orange — only the root:

  The colour — 0xff008cff = RGB(255,140,0), against the diff bar's
  RGB(255,170,85): the white taken out, so it reads orange rather than apricot.
  It lives in mexpru beside the diff bar's constant (mexpru.DECL_NAME_COLOR),
  since two files paint with it.

  Only the root — in f(x) the f goes orange; the argument, brackets, and every
  free letter stay default. A bare declaration's reference wears it too (the f
  in the undeclared-style product), since that is equally a use of a
  declaration.

  When — exactly where you said: during/after validation. The paint is part of
  the parse that the lock, a gesture, or a paste already runs — no continuous
  verifying was added. And because the parse owns it end to end (each parse
  first resets every symbol to the default, then paints the roots that resolve),
  the orange is a fact of the current declarations: delete the definition above
  and re-lock, and the name loses it. The diff bar is not the parse's to touch
  — rules are left alone, and a differential keeps its bar through a reparse
  (asserted).

  The distributed child (born locked, no parse of its own) keeps the colour by
  two roads, both tested:

  - a declared-name reference is cloned on write, and a clone now carries a
    glyph's colour — a copy of a declared name is still a use of it
  - a call's callee letters are built fresh by the writer, so it paints them as
    it builds

  One honest limit, same as the tagging rule: a quoted multi-glyph name
  ('max_lim') doesn't paint — its root is several glyphs and colouring just one
  would misstate the name. Restart the app to see it.

● Update(DESIGN.md)
  ⎿  Added 4 lines, removed 1 line
      76  ## Derivatives are marked fractions    (2026-09-15)
      77  
      78  A derivative IS a fraction in notation and stays one on the mex
          pr side: the fraction's own
      79 -`u.diff` is the only stored fact - drawn as an ORANGE bar, the 
         -one visual difference - and every                              
      79 +`u.diff` is the only stored fact - drawn as a GREEN bar, the on
         +e visual difference - and every                                
      80  semantic (the order, the signs, the variables, the binding) is 
          re-derived from the glyphs at each
      81  parse. The d's are plain letter glyphs with nothing on them; `d
          ` may never name a variable inside
      82  a derivative, so every d-unit is a sign by inspection and `d^2d
          ` reads determinately.

● 100/100. Three things landed:

  Why f wasn't orange — the box was loaded, not touched: a loaded document's
  boxes are rebuilt by from_latex and nothing ever parsed them, and the paint
  lives in the parse. deserialize now runs the validation parse once per formula
  box at load — your exact formula paints both f's (0xff008cff, verified in the
  harness before the fix). One parse per box, at load only; nothing continuous.

  The load tests the lock — per your ruling: a locked box whose tree no longer
  parses comes back unlocked with all that means — parent link dropped, children
  pruned (the same consequences the lock button's unlock carries; the pruning
  runs after the walk so indices don't shift under it). A chain that still
  parses survives intact, and an unlocked box is never tested — it wasn't
  claiming trust. Tested in test_load_lock.lua: @@ saved locked loads back
  unlocked and childless; a(b+c) → ab+ac keeps its lock and its link.

  The derivative is emerald — 0xff50c878 (RGB 80,200,120), the classic emerald.
  This also cleanly separates the two marks, which I suspect was the point:
  names orange, derivatives green. The change is one constant (DIFF_BAR_COLOR),
  with every comment and DESIGN.md entry that said "orange bar" corrected to
  green — DESIGN.md's entry records the reuleing with its date.

  Restart the app; a fresh load of your document should show f orange in both
  terms and the d/dx bar green.

✻ Cogitated for 4m 22s · done 1:39 AM

❯ delete inside an locked formula does nothing to the text like it should, but  
  "refreshes" the formula and the variables become white again, which I don't   
  like, it why does it do that?                                                 

  Thought for 20s (ctrl+o to expand)
● The "why" is in the locked branch of handle_input
  (scripts/editor_formula.lua): an edit inside a locked box is discarded by 
  rebuilding the tree from the committed text — and from_latex hands back a
  brand-new tree whose glyphs carry default colors. The orange lived on the old
  tree's glyphs (the parse painted them), so the discard throws it away with the
  edit. Nothing re-parses afterward, so the box sits white until its next
  validation (a gesture re-parses and re-paints).

  The fix follows the same rule as the load: any path that replaces a locked
  box's tree re-runs the validation parse. The decls parameter is already in
  handle_input's signature — reserved, per its own manifest, "for this file's
  future needs"; this is that need:

  Thought for 3s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Fixed — 100/100. To recap the "why": delete inside a locked box lands the edit
  and then discards it by rebuilding the tree from the committed text (that's
  the recorded no-key-list design — "did the tree change" rather than
  enumerating editing keys). But from_latex returns a brand-new tree: the orange
  lived on the old glyphs, painted there by the parse, so the discard threw the
  paint away with the edit — and nothing re-parsed, so the box sat white until
  your next right-click (a gesture re-parses, which is why it came back then).

  The fix is the same rule the load now follows, stated in the comment: whatever
  replaces a locked box's tree runs the validation once after. The locked
  branch now re-parses the rebuilt tree with the document's declarations — which
  restores the orange and the click-resolution tags the gestures use (those
  lived on the old glyphs too; you'd have been one gesture away from stale
  ones). Fittingly, handle_input's decls parameter was already in the signature,
  documented as "reserved for this file's future needs" — this was the need;
  the manifest and @param now say what it actually does.

  One honest note on verification: the suite can't drive this exact path —
  synthesizing an edit keystroke needs real input events the headless harness
  doesn't produce (a known gap, same as the draw path). What's tested is the
  mechanism the fix calls: a fresh from_latex tree parsed with declarations
  paints its declared names. The wiring is three lines on top of that. Restart
  and try the delete — the refresh flicker stays (that's the rebuild doing its
  job), but the names should stay orange through it.

✻ Brewed for 1m 8s · done 1:41 AM

❯ maybe deleted children should actually turn into a text box, containing all   
  their formulas, one on a line                                                 
   (that's the recorded no-key-list design -- what no-key-list design?          

  Thought for 19s (ctrl+o to expand)

● The "no-key-list design" — my phrase for something recorded in
  editor_formula.lua's own header and branch comment, not in DESIGN.md. The
  locked box could enforce immutability two ways:

  - block the keys that edit — which means maintaining a list of "editing keys"
    (Backspace, Delete, letters, brackets, ...), and every time a new editing
    key is added to the app, the list is one entry from going stale and silently
    letting an edit through;
  - or let all input through — so selection, navigation and copy keep working in
    a locked box — and ask one question afterward: did the tree change? If it
    did, rebuild from the committed text, and whatever the key was, the edit is
    gone.

  The file took the second (its words: "Enumerating 'which keys are edits' would
  be a list to forget an entry from; asking 'did the tree change' cannot be").
  That's the mechanism you just watched: delete lands, the tree changes, the
  rebuild discards it. It's a comment-recorded rationale from 2026-09-08 — a
  design the code carries, not one you ruled into DESIGN.md. If you want it
  ruled (or changed), say so and I'll record it properly.

  Deleted children becoming a text box — I like the shape of this: pruning stops
  being destruction and becomes demotion — the children leave the derivation
  but stay in the document as content. Sketch of how it would work, before I
  touch anything:

  - the change lives in content.prune_descendants, the one place children are
    dropped today (unlock, paste-into-formula, and the load's lock-test failure
    all route through it)
  - instead of removing each pruned box, collect the whole subtree in document
    order and replace it with one text box at the first pruned child's position
  - each child's formula goes into that text box on its own line, as a real 
    inline formula — still a formula object you can select, copy and re-promote,
    not LaTeX text — with the box's latex kept as the fallback for anything
    that can't be rebuilt
  - grandchildren included, in order, one per line

  One decision is yours before I'd start: real formulas in the text box (my
  recommendation — they keep their ink and their copyability, and "one on a
  line" is just a newline between them) or LaTeX text lines (cheaper, but the
  formulas stop being formulas). Want it built, and which?

  Thought for 13s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● 100/100. The whitelist is in, and it's the shape you described — LOCKED_ALLOW
  in editor_formula.lua, a set the funnel must pass before it runs:

  - on the list today: all movement (nav.left/right/up/down, the sprints, the
    structural backs), all selection (select_left/right, select_all), and copy —
    copy rides with selection since a locked formula was always copyable, which
    the mid-chain dance needs
  - everything else is refused at the door: the input funnel isn't even called,
    so the tree cannot change — delete, backspace and typing now do nothing: no
    edit, no discard-rebuild, no refresh flicker, and no repaint to lose. The
    bug you reported two messages ago is dead at the root rather than patched.
  - "additions are a feature": granting more is literally adding one line to the
    set. A typo'd id fails safe — that action is refused like any other, never
    silently admitted.

  Two things the gate needed that weren't obvious:

  1. Typed characters bypass keymap entirely (they arrive through the ImGui
     character queue), so "no action fired" alone would have let letters
     through. The gate also requires an empty character queue.
  2. Keymap could answer "did this action fire" but not "what fired" — which is
     the question a subset-admitting gate has to ask. New keymap.fired() answers
     it, iterating the registry once.

  Fallout cleaned up: handle_input no longer needs the decls parameter (nothing
  rebuilds anymore), so the signature, manifest, and content's call site all
  shrank. The file header's old "did the tree change" rationale is rewritten to
  your ruling, and DESIGN.md's lock entry now records it in your words —
  including the "additions to the list are a feature not fixing a bug" reasoning
  — alongside the load-tests-every-lock rule.

  Same honest note as before: the keystroke path itself needs live input the
  harness can't synthesize; the suite pins the contracts (signatures, manifest)
  at 100/100. Restart and try — delete in a locked box should now be a true
  no-op, arrows and selection still alive.

❯ arrows don't work inside the locked formula                                   

  Thought for 1m 25s, searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

● keymap.fired() over-reports: radial.text is bound to Up, radial.formula to
  Left, radial.definition to Right, help.prev/next_chapter to Left/Right — every
  arrow press fires its movement action plus a radial/help twin on the same
  key. Those twins are inert while a box owns the frame (the radial and panels
  route exclusively, and are closed), but the gate saw them as non-whitelisted
  and refused everything.

  The fix keeps your polarity: the gate counts only actions that are the
  mathbox's own — the nav. / math. / text. / edit. prefixes, which are exactly
  what the funnel reads. Context twins outside those prefixes aren't this box's
  business. And it fails safe in the right direction: a new funnel action
  follows those prefixes by convention, so it's refused until you whitelist it —
  additions stay a feature, never a hole:

● 100/100 — three things landed this turn:

  Reload paints without re-locking — the root cause of your report: a loaded
  definition holds only its slots; the name parse that makes it serve 
  declarations lives in the draw path. So the formula boxes' load-parse ran
  against empty declarations and painted nothing. deserialize now calls a new
  editor_definition.refresh on every definition first — pattern-only, 
  deliberately not the draw's sync: the arity reshape is the live box's
  business, and a body saved with an off-shape slot count must load byte-exact
  (my first attempt ran the full sync and broke the round-trip test exactly that
  way — caught and narrowed).

  Deleted children are collected, not lost — prune_descendants (the one funnel
  behind unlock, the mid-chain dance, paste-into-formula, and the load's
  lock-test failure) now gathers the doomed formulas in document order and
  inserts one text box in place of the first deleted child, its body in the
  editor's own $$LaTeX$$ inline form — so the formulas come back as real formula
  objects, one per line, selectable and copyable like any prose formula. The
  derivation links are still properly deleted: the text box holds content, not
  chain. Tested end to end (ab+ac survives as $$ab+ac$$ in a text box; the
  mid-chain test now asserts the grandchild's collection).

  The integral's d is green — the closing d of the \int…d pair paints with the
  differential family's emerald at validation, same as the derivative's bar; the
  \int sign itself stays default. The color is now one exported constant,
  mexpru.DIFF_COLOR, shared by mark_diff and the parse. One consequence I
  handled: a bracket half is how that d is drawn, so zoom/undo's rebuild now
  carries a half's color like any glyph's — a green d survives a zoom.

  Restart and try: reload should show orange names and green bars/d's
  immediately, unlocking a chain should leave the steps behind as a text box of
  formulas, and \int_0^1 x \,dx should close on a green d.

  Thought for 7s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● 100/100. The why: for a declared name with a subscript, the row slot holds the
  name and its sub — and the sub is the name's argument (k in \vec{F}_{k} is
  what \vec{F} is applied to), never part of the name itself. The paint walked
  that whole slot, so k came along.

  The fix is in leaf_drawn_by — the one rule that says which glyph is "the leaf"
  of a slot. It already knew a superscript rides on a slot without belonging to
  it (the base alone is the leaf); its comment claimed a subscript can never
  arrive there, which was true when written but stopped being true the moment
  declared names with sub-arguments started resolving. A sub now gets the same
  treatment: the leaf is the base under it. So \vec{F}_{k} paints exactly the F
  — the vec arrow and the k stay as they were.

  The same rule feeds the writer's copy tags and the click-resolution tags, so
  both now point at the name's own glyph rather than a subtree containing the
  argument — a distributed copy of a declared reference won't try to drag
  someone else's subscript along as the name's ink.

  Pinned in test_decl_paint: \vec{F}_{k} against a declared \vec{F}_{m} — F
  orange, k default. Full suite 100/100; restart to see it.

● Both landed — the shackle is a real arc now, and the colors are reversed:

  The arc — the miswrite was in the original trick: the shackle was drawn as a
  full circle with the body's fill meant to cover its lower half. That worked
  while the body was filled, but the hollow body (the unlocked state) let the
  whole ring show. ImGui's draw list has no AddArc, so the leaf wrapper
  (imgui_composer.h, which is mine to edit) gained one — ImGui_AddArc, built on
  PathArcTo/PathStroke — and the padlock now draws the upper semicircle directly
  in both states. No covering trick left to break.

  The colors, reversed per your ruling — locked = green, closed and filled
  ("verified"); unlocked = red, open and hollow. The comment on LOCK_GREEN
  records both rulings with the reversal, so nobody "fixes" it back.

  One practical note: imgui_composer.h changed, which is C++ — the Lua side
  calls vc.ImGui_AddArc, and that symbol only exists after a rebuild. The test
  harness rebuilt itself in the run above (100/100), but your main.exe needs a 
  make before the padlock draws at all.

❯ I can no longer scrol, I think your arc change somehow broke it, can you look 
  at the logs maybe?                                                            

  Thought for 19s, listed 1 directory (ctrl+o to expand)

● No Lua errors in the log — but that tail tells the story: "Ctrl+R: Lua 
  reloaded" — the Lua side was reloaded without the executable being rebuilt.
  ImGui_AddArc lives in C++ (imgui_composer.h); an unbuilt exe doesn't have it,
  so vc.ImGui_AddArc is nil and every frame's draw dies at the padlock — killing
  the layout (and the scroll with it). Checking the timestamps:

  Thought for 7s, listed 1 directory (ctrl+o to expand)

● Confirmed: main.exe is from Sep 14 — two days before the arc export was
  written into the header today. The Lua reloaded fine and calls
  vc.ImGui_AddArc, which doesn't exist in the old binary — every frame's draw
  throws at the padlock and the rest of the frame (layout, scroll) dies. Not the
  arc code; a stale exe. Rebuilding it (building only — never launching):

  Thought for 4s, listed 1 directory, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Rebuilt and verified — main.exe is now from today and carries the ImGui_AddArc
  export (the name is findable in the binary). Restart the app and scrolling
  should be back.

  What the log showed: no errors at all — just Ctrl+R: Lua reloaded. That was
  the trap: Ctrl+R reloads the Lua scripts, never the executable. The arc export
  lives in C++ (imgui_composer.h, the leaf I'm allowed to edit), your exe was
  from Sep 14, and the freshly reloaded content.lua called a function the old
  binary never had — so every frame died at the lock button, silently, before
  the layout and scroll ever ran. The suite couldn't catch it either: the
  harness was rebuilt as part of its run, which is exactly why it stayed green
  while your app broke.

  Rule of thumb going forward: anything that touches imgui_composer.h (or any
  .h/.cpp) needs a make plus a restart — a Ctrl+R will happily load Lua that
  calls into a binary that can't answer.

  differential marks appear at different moments. The derivative's bar goes
  green the instant ctrl+/ upgrades the fraction — creation-time ink — while the
  integral's d waits for validation. If you'd like the d green from the moment

✻ Worked for 1m 53s · done 3:01 AM

❯ i would like after an edit if possible to revalidate it, I am thinking not to 
  validate it on lock, but on any change, how would that be? and in this way    
  any formula would validate whenever                                           

  Thought for 36s (ctrl+o to expand)

● I like it — and it's distinct from the thing you ruled out earlier: that was
  continuous verification (a parse every frame, watching for changes). This is
  event-driven: the version bump an edit already makes is the event, so the
  parse runs once per keystroke, not per frame. Nothing watches anything. Here's
  how it would hang together:

  The hook — the EDIT-mode commit in editor_formula.handle_input already does
  push_undo + latex = to_latex on every real edit. The revalidation slots in
  right there: one ast_gestures.ast_for call with the document's declarations.
  Routing through ast_for rather than a bare parse means the gesture layer's
  cache is seeded for free — the right-click menu reads the same parse instead
  of redoing it.

  What you'd see while typing — names turn orange the moment they resolve, the
  integral's d goes green the keystroke it closes, and both flicker off while
  the formula is temporarily unparseable (f( mid-typing) and come back when it
  reads again — the paint honestly tracks "does this resolve right now".

  Lock stops parsing — per your point: with every change validated, the lock's
  own parse is redundant. lock() would read the standing verdict instead of
  running its own check — same restriction (an unparseable tree still can't
  lock, that ruling stands), zero cost, because the verdict is already cached.
  The load seeds the cache too (deserialize routing its formula-box parse
  through the same ast_for), undo/redo revalidate after restoring, and paste's
  verification is unchanged.

  Cost — one parse per keystroke, same order of work as the to_latex the commit
  already does. Formulas are small; this is not the per-frame walk you rejected.

  One consequence worth naming: with the parse always fresh, the distinction
  "this box has been validated" stops being a moment and becomes a standing
  state — the lock's color then means trust, not "has a tree", which is what the
  padlock's green already says.

  Want it built? The pieces: the commit hook (+ decls returning to
  handle_input), the lock's verdict read, the load seeding, undo/redo
  revalidation, and the DESIGN.md record of the reversal.

  Thought for 7s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● 100/100 — both halves landed.

  The palette (all parse-side, one helper shared by the three binders):

  - blue 0xffffaf50 = a linked variable — the dx's x, every apparition caught 
    inside the scope (the integrand's x), the derivative's denominator variables
    and their body mentions, and the big operators' spawned names (the k=1
    constraint and the F_k's k — your sum formula from earlier now reads blue
    throughout)
  - green = structural: the diff bar, the integral's closing d — the d's glyph
    is the mark, the x beside it is the link, so dx wears both colors
  - orange = globals (declared names); plain undeclared letters stay default

  The walk is scoped to each binder's own ink — a global named x elsewhere keeps
  its orange, and inside a scope where the binder shadows it, blue is the
  honest reading anyway.

  On-change validation — per your "yeah": the edit's own version bump is the
  event; the commit revalidates once per keystroke through ast_for (never
  per-frame — the "don't continuously verify" ruling stands against polling, and
  DESIGN.md now records both rulings side by side). The lock no longer parses —
  it reads the standing verdict, same restriction, zero cost. The load seeds
  the same cache (so a right-click after reload is free too), and undo/redo
  revalidate like any change.

  What you'll see while typing: names turn orange as they resolve, bound
  variables go blue the keystroke they bind — and both honestly flicker off
  while the formula is mid-word and doesn't read yet. All Lua this time, so no
  rebuild — restart the app and type.

● Fixed — 100/100. The bug was mine, and small: the denominator-painting loop
  tested only is_letter, and d is a letter — so the differential signs slipped
  through the very test meant to exclude them and painted blue (with "d" even
  entering the linked-name set, which is the "passed downwards" you spotted).
  The loop now applies the same sign test the variable collection right above it
  has always used: d and \partial are signs in this context, letters beyond
  them are variables.

  Your exact formula is pinned in the test: both d signs stay plain — the green
  bar already says what the fraction is — while the denominator's x and the
  body's x inside a(x+y) wear the bound blue, which is the apparition rule
  working as you ruled it. Lua only; restart and the d's should read plain.

  Thought for 8s, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● 100/100 — reasonable, and it turned out clean because of a fact the reload
  already relies on: Ctrl+R saves before reloading, so the view rides the save
  format and comes back through the same door. That means a restart (or a
  crash-then-open) restores your place too, not just a reload.

  The format gained one final record after the boxes:

  view <scroll> <active> <caret>

  - scroll — the document's scroll position, clamped non-negative; the first
    draw clamps it against the real height anyway
  - active — which box you were in
  - caret — and since the text editor's caret is one plain index into its chars,
    the active text box's caret comes along for free. A formula box's caret
    still resets to the row's end as before — say the word if you want that
    carried too

  Two safety properties, both pinned in tests: a box body can contain any bytes,
  but bodies are consumed by length and never scanned, so nothing in your
  content can fake or swallow the record — and a file without one (every older
  save) loads exactly as it always did. A stale view (a box pruned at load, a
  hand-edited file) falls back to box 1 rather than an index into nothing.

  All Lua — restart, and the next Ctrl+R should land you where you were: same
  scroll, same box, same caret.

      544 +@date 2026-09-16 16:00 ]]                                     
      545 +local function emit_int(ctx_write, node)                      
      546 +    local open = glyph_op(ctx_write, "\\int")                 
      547 +    if not open then                                          
      548 +        return nil, "no glyph for \\int in the font"          
      549 +    end                                                       
      550 +    local close = glyph(ctx_write, "d")                       
      551 +    if not close then                                         
      552 +        return nil, "no glyph for d in the font"              
      553 +    end                                                       
      554 +    -- THE PAIR, the same peer links `bracketed` builds: the r
          +eparse finds the close BY PEER.                               
      555 +    mexpru.u(open).bracket = {is_open = true, type = char.BRAC
          +KET_INTEGRAL,                                                 
      556 +            peer = mexpru.u(close)}

      561 +    --[[ The bounds ride the halves at the size a typed supsub
          + uses, rebound the way emit_power

          + not the row's. A NULL bound is no                            
      563 +    side at all. ]]

          +write.origins,

      575 +        return mexpru.horiz(ctx_write.fs, run, sup_sz)

      580 +    end

● All three reports are fixed (101/101), and the blink is a main.cpp change —
  core, so here's the suggestion for you to apply rather than an edit:

  Where: the reload block at main.cpp:333-345 (vs = fresh → test_init →
  DBG("Ctrl+R: Lua reloaded")). What: record a deadline beside reload_armed and
  let the frame loop skip drawing until it passes:

  // beside `static bool reload_armed = false;` (line 300)
  static std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point reload_blink_until{};

  // after `DBG("Ctrl+R: Lua reloaded");` (line 344)
  reload_blink_until = std::chrono::steady_clock::now() +
  std::chrono::milliseconds(200);

  and at the draw call site in the frame loop (wherever test_draw is invoked):

  // A reload BLINKS: 200ms of blank frames after the swap, so the reload is 
  visible - the Lua-side
  // rebuild is instant and flicker-free, which is exactly why it needs a 
  deliberate signal.
  bool blinking = std::chrono::steady_clock::now() < reload_blink_until;
  if (!blinking) {
      auto [ret, err] = vc::call_lua<int>(vs.get(), "test_draw", /* ... */);
      // ...
  }

  Presenting still happens every frame, so the screen goes genuinely blank for
  the 200ms rather than freezing on the last old frame — clear, then return,
  which reads as a blink. One nuance worth keeping: skip drawing only — keep
  ImGui's frame plumbing and the input pipe alive through the blank window, or
  the reload's own key-release (reload_armed) behavior changes.

  Now the three fixes, all verified:

  1. F4 crash — NODE_LABEL never had an INT entry; the INT branch's very first
     line(NODE_LABEL[t]) got nil and died on any integral, your
     sum-inside-integral just being the first one anyone pressed F4 on. Entry
     added, comment records the assumption.
  2. Alt+Shift+Q inside a locked formula — the glyph families (Greek,
     Alt+punctuation, the glyph map) are raw ImGui polls by design, not keymap
     actions, so the whitelist's universe didn't contain them: no action fired,
     no character queued, gate open. The gate now refuses Alt-without-Ctrl
     frames unless a whitelisted action fired — which keeps Alt-movement
     (math.back_up and kin) working, since those arrive as actions.
  3. Distribute on your formula — offered, applied, and then the write refused
     cannot write a INT back yet: the writer had no integral. emit_int now
     builds the real pair — \int with its size boost, bounds beside, integrand,
     closing d with the peer link (the reparse refuses an unpaired integral, so
     this is what makes the round trip hold), variable cloned from the glyph the
     parse now tags, carrying its blue, the d painted green. Your exact formula
     distributes, writes, and round-trips; pinned in test_int_write.lua along
     with definite and indefinite forms.

  All Lua — restart and all three should behave.

      358 +0xAABBGGRR packing. @date 2026-09-16 ]]

      360 +local LINK_THICK  = 1.0                                       
      361 +local LINK_SEGS   = 24                                        
      362 +                                                              
      363 +--[[ @brief The parabola that arcs from `a` to `b`, tangent to
          + the ceiling above them.                                      
      364 +-- |                                                          
      365 +-- | THE AUTHOR'S OWN SPECIFICATION (2026-09-16), verbatim in 
          +substance: "a parabola hiting 3                               
      366 +-- | points: 1. the variable declaration, 2. the ceiling of th
          +e formula box and 3. the reference                            
      367 +-- | ... the third point, the one on the ceiling should be as 
          +such that the ceiling is also                                 
      368 +-- | tangent to the parabola". A horizontal tangent is the VER
          +TEX, so the ceiling point is the                              
      369 +-- | parabola's vertex: y = ceiling + c(x - h)^2 for the h and
          + c that pass through both ends.                               
      370 +-- |                                                          
      371 +-- | SOLVED IN SCREEN COORDINATES, y growing downward, so "bel
          +ow the ceiling" is the larger y and                           
      372 +-- | the arms hang from the vertex. With the ends' depths dA a
          +nd dB below the ceiling, the vertex                           
      373 +-- | sits BETWEEN them (u and v of opposite sign), which fixes
          + the ratio u/v = -sqrt(dA/dB) and                             
      374 +-- | with it h; equal depths give the symmetric midpoint, as t
          +hey should.                                                   
      375 +-- |                                                          
      376 +-- | @param a        {x, y} - one end, at the top of the decla
          +ring glyph                                                    
      377 +-- | @param b        {x, y} - the other end, at the top of the
          + reference glyph                                              
      378 +-- | @param ceiling  number - the y of the box's top edge; the
          + arc touches it and never crosses                             
      379 +-- | @param n        integer - how many segments to sample the
          + curve into                                                   
      380 +-- | @return {{x, y}, ...} | nil - the points from `a` to `b`,
          + in order; nil when no parabola                               
      381 +-- |         exists (ends at the same x, or an end not below t
          +he ceiling)                                                   
      382 +-- |                                                          
      383 +-- | @date 2026-09-16                                         
      384 +--]]                                                          
      385 +function editor.link_arc(a, b, ceiling, n)                    
      386 +    if math.abs(a.x - b.x) < 1e-6 then                        
      387 +        return nil                                            
      388 +    end                                                       
      389 +    local left, right = a, b                                  
      390 +    if left.x > right.x then                                  
      391 +        left, right = b, a                                    
      392 +    end                                                       
      393 +    local d_left, d_right = left.y - ceiling, right.y - ceilin
          +g                                                             
      394 +    if d_left <= 0 or d_right <= 0 then                       
      395 +        return nil                                            
      396 +    end                                                       
      397 +    local ratio = math.sqrt(d_left / d_right)                 
      398 +    local v = (right.x - left.x) / (ratio + 1)                
      399 +    local h = right.x - v                                     
      400 +    local c = d_left / ((left.x - h) * (left.x - h))          
      401 +    local pts = {}                                            
      402 +    for k = 0, n do                                           
      403 +        local x = left.x + (right.x - left.x) * k / n         
      404 +        pts[#pts + 1] = {x = x, y = ceiling + c * (x - h) * (x
          + - h)}                                                        
      405 +    end                                                       
      406 +    return pts                                                
      407 +end                                                           
      408 +                                                              
      409 +--[[ The top-centre of the glyph `node` draws at, ABOVE ITS DE
          +CORATOR when it has one: the link's                           
      410 +end is the outermost ink of the name, an accent included. The 
          +box is the tree frame's; the caller                           
      411 +holds the draw origin. @date 2026-09-16 ]]                    
      412 +local function glyph_top(fontset, node)                       
      413 +    local outer = node                                        
      414 +    local parent = outer:get_parent()                         
      415 +    if parent and mexpru.u(parent) and mexpru.u(parent).kind =
          += "dress" then                                                
      416 +        outer = parent                                        
      417 +    end                                                       
      418 +    local box = mformula.node_bbox(fontset, outer)            
      419 +    return {x = (box.left + box.right) / 2, y = box.top}      
      420 +end                                                           
      421 +                                                              
      422 +--[[ @brief Draws the bound-variable links, each an arc from d
          +eclaration to reference.                                      
      423 +-- |                                                          
      424 +-- | BEHIND THE FORMULA BY CALL ORDER (the author, 2026-09-16:
          + "an arc behind the formula                                   
      425 +-- | drawing"): the caller issues this before it draws the gly
          +phs, and ImGui has no depth - what                            
      426 +-- is drawn first is behind. The links come from the container
          +'s own parse (`_bound_links`,                                 
      427 +-- refreshed by every parse); a container nothing has parsed h
          +as none, and so has one whose last                            
      428 +-- parse failed.                                              
      429 +-- |                                                          
      430 +-- | @param container  mexpru.container - checked; read for it
          +s links and glyph boxes                                       
      431 +-- | @param fontset    fontset                                
      432 +-- | @param draw_x     number - the origin the formula is draw
          +n at this frame                                               
      433 +-- | @param draw_y     number - the baseline it is drawn at   
      434 +-- | @param ceiling    number - SCREEN y of the box's top edge
          + - the arcs' tangent line                                     
      435 +-- | @return nothing                                          
      436 +-- |                                                          
      437 +-- | @date 2026-09-16                                         
      438 +--]]                                                          
      439 +function editor.draw_bound_links(container, fontset, draw_x, d
          +raw_y, ceiling)                                               
      440 +    mexpru.check_container(container)                         
      441 +    local links = container._bound_links                      
      442 +    if not links then                                         
      443 +        return                                                
      444 +    end                                                       
      445 +    for _, link in pairs(links) do                            
      446 +        local a = glyph_top(fontset, link.decl)               
      447 +        for _, ref in ipairs(link.refs) do                    
      448 +            local b = glyph_top(fontset, ref)                 
      449 +            local pts = editor.link_arc(a, b, ceiling, LINK_SE
          +GS)                                                           
      450 +            if pts then                                       
      451 +                for k = 2, #pts do                            
      452 +                    vc.ImGui_AddLine(                         
      453 +                            {x = draw_x + pts[k - 1].x, y = dr
          +aw_y + pts[k - 1].y},                                         
      454 +                            {x = draw_x + pts[k].x, y = draw_y
          + + pts[k].y},                                                 
      455 +                            LINK_COLOR, LINK_THICK)           
      456 +                end                                           
      457 +            end                                               
      458 +        end                                                   
      459 +    end                                                       
      460 +end                                                           
      461 +                                                              
      462  return editor

● 102/102 — everything landed. Recap of the two:

  The blink (applied to main.cpp as authorized, exe rebuilt): after a successful
  reload, 200ms of skipped Lua drawing — but the pipe pump, ImGui plumbing and
  rendering keep running, so the screen goes blank-and-back rather than
  freezing, and a command arriving mid-blink still lands.

  The arcs, built exactly to your definition:

  - The curve — a horizontal tangent is a vertex, which is what makes your third
    point determinate: the ceiling point is the parabola's vertex, so the arc
    touches the ceiling once and never crosses. Both glyph-top endpoints are hit
    exactly; with unequal depths the vertex slides toward the shallower end;
    ends at the same x (or not below the ceiling) honestly draw nothing. Solved
    and sampled in screen coordinates, 24 segments.
  - The ends — the top-centre of each glyph, taken above the decorator when the
    glyph is dressed (the accent's box, not the letter's).
  - Behind — issued before the glyph drawing in the same frame; ImGui has no
    depth, so first-drawn is behind.
  - What links — the parse itself records, per bound variable, its declaration
    glyph and every reference glyph (_bound_links on the container, refreshed by
    each parse, dropped by a failed one — also now declared in the container's
    seal, after your live error taught me that). The integral's dx x and the
    derivative's denominator letters are the declarations; the body's caught
    mentions are the references. A global or free letter has no declaration
    glyph, so it forms no link — no binder list needed. Color: the bound blue at
    a ghost's opacity, so it reads as annotation, not ink.

  Tests pin the geometry (ends hit, ceiling touched-not-crossed, symmetric
  midpoint, asymmetric slide, the nil cases) and the link formation (\int x\,dx
  → one declaration + one reference; a+b → nothing). One honest gap: sums and 
  the other big operators don't arc yet — their "declaration" is also a
  reference (the k in k=0), and tagging it as the declaration would cost that
  glyph its click-resolution. If you want them linked, that tradeoff is yours to
  call.

  Both the exe and the Lua changed — restart and Ctrl+R should blink, and a
  locked ∫ x\,dx or d/dx should show blue arcs from the differential to its
  mentions.

● 103/103. Two answers:

  The arcs — two faults, both mine, both fixed:

  1. The frame — draw() hands pos.y + baseline_correction(fontset, sz) to the
     C++, not the plain baseline: glyphs are re-centred on the vertical middle
     of 'a', and everything reading node boxes must undo/redo that same shift.
     My arcs used the raw baseline and landed a half-line off the formula —
     drawn, but not where you'd ever see them as this box's arcs. The correction
     is now exported (mformula_new.baseline_correction, documented as what it
     is) and applied.
  2. The order — they were also issued before the field's background fill, which
     washed over them. Now: fill first, arcs, then glyphs — genuinely behind
     the formula.

  The lag — I chased it honestly and couldn't convict anything by reading:
  selection does not trigger the on-change validation (I verified every version
  bump site — only real tree edits, mark_edited; a selection move pays no
  parse), and the two new per-frame paths both look sub-millisecond (the links
  walk early-outs on unparsed boxes and costs a few box queries when links
  exist; the whitelist's keymap.fired() is ~200 cheap ImGui polls, and only for
  the active locked box). So instead of guessing, I instrumented both: profiler
  scopes lua.draw.bound_links and lua.input.locked_funnel now exist alongside
  the older lua.draw.mexpr_draw.

  Restart (Lua only), open F3, drag a selection around, and read which scope
  eats the frame — if one of mine is the cost, the number will say so and I'll
  fix the named thing; if it's an older scope (mexpr_draw, the hit-test
  descent), we've been staring at a pre-existing cost that the arc-hunting just
  surfaced. Ctrl+F3 records worst frames to perf_spikes.log if you'd rather send
  me the file.
